# Correction ledger — frequency-weighted reinforcement

Design per Kam (2026-08-03, voice): *"similar to when people learn, frequency is
used for reinforcement — if a mistake happens once, that could be isolated; if it
happens more than once, an increased weight is given."*

## The weight scale

Weight = number of occurrences. Each recurrence escalates the response:

| w | Meaning | Required response |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Isolated** — could be noise | Log it. Write a lesson file only if it would change future behaviour (importance filter). |
| 2 | **Reinforced** — a pattern, not noise | Lesson file mandatory. If one existed and didn't fire, diagnose *why* (wrong file? too abstract? bad retrieval handle?) and fix the lesson, not just the mistake. |
| ≥3 | **Regression** — the system is failing to learn | Treat like a failing test (no-skip rule). Automatic promotion candidate: move the rule into `identity/` or into *enforcement* (launcher check, hook, CLAUDE.md line). Raise it with Kam at the next briefing. |

Rules:
- Same *underlying* mistake in a new costume still increments the weight — match
  on root cause, not surface form.
- Weights never decay automatically. They are only retired at weekly
  consolidation, with the reasoning written in the audit note.
- Positive reinforcement counts too: when Kam explicitly praises a behaviour,
  log it as `praise` — repetition there tells us what to *keep* doing.

Both refusals red-proofed against a genuinely dirty tree. | w=2 with the panel_sync deadlock (same file, same class: a guard whose failure mode is indefinite silence) | Fixed, red-proofed, daemon restarted and confirmed `ppid 1`. I have enumerated nothing and run no command to do so; this brief commissions that enumeration rather than asserting its result."* 🔑 **The gate caught me writing a classification I had inherited from a CARD rather than measured** — the w=9 family (`"board config, reversible"` on a workflow shared by 25 schemes) pointed at myself, on the same day I was correcting the Secuura seat for the same shape. **In-path enforcement works on its author too, which is the whole argument for building it in the path rather than in a rule.** | family w=9, and this is a CATCH not a miss | No lesson owed — the mechanism exists and fired. | 2026-09-10 | 🔴 **I PUT A FACT IN FRONT OF KAM THAT DECIDED HIS CHOICE, AND IT WAS FALSE — a WRONG-KEY FALSE ZERO relayed as a finding.** My card said `@prisma/config` and `prisma` were "genuinely untriaged, in NEITHER allow-list", and that framing is why he picked `one-list-triage-two` over `populate-ci-list`. **All 6/6 advisories beneath them were already accepted.** The seat had grepped the baseline by PACKAGE NAME; **the baseline is keyed by GHSA advisory id and describes advisories, so a carrier package correctly never appears** — the query could not have matched whatever was in the file, and it returned "nothing" rather than "wrong question". ⚠ **AND CI'S OWN OUTPUT HAD ALREADY SAID SO — it printed `advisory id(s) (no source id)` for exactly those two, and NEITHER OF US READ IT.** 🔑 **I RELAYED IT WITHOUT ASKING WHAT KEY THE STORE IS INDEXED BY.** The seat's zero came with no statement of its own key, and I carded it as a finding — the representations family, in the costume where the missing provenance is about the SHAPE of the query rather than its source. ✅ **The seat self-corrected within the hour, unprompted, with the 6/6 table and a FABRICATED GHSA key as the control proving the reader works** — that control is what makes a retraction spendable. ✅ **Kam's choice SURVIVES on a structural fact neither of us held: `check-npm-audit.mjs` requires a numeric `advisory_id` per entry, so a "(no source id)" package can NEVER be allow-listed there — `populate-ci-list` could not have worked.** Told him the fact was wrong, the option was right, and the reasoning under it changed. **RULE: before a zero means anything, state what KEY the store is indexed by and confirm the query uses it.** | **w=18, cap/undercount+false-absence family** — second instance TODAY of me relaying a number I had not interrogated (the `per_page=100` row is the first) | Told Kam same session, unprompted, leading with the fact being wrong rather than with the option surviving. Enforcement candidate stands: nothing guards a hand-written query's KEY, and `board_count.sh` guards only totals. |
| 2026-09-10 | 🟡 **MY INSTRUCTION TO THE SEAT WAS WRONG AND IT PUSHED BACK INSTEAD OF FOLLOWING IT — I told it to "make `tsx` resolvable", which would have turned a gate green by REMOVING THE WARNING rather than fixing the defect.** The harness message *"the tsx driver did not run"* is **wrong about its own failure**: the driver ran and returned the correct `NULL`. The real mechanism is `probe="$(… 2>&1)"` — **stderr folded INTO the value being compared to `"NULL"`**, so on a clean runner npx's install warning concatenates and the comparison fails. I reasoned from the harness's own message rather than from the code, and specified a fix that buys a green by silencing the evidence. ✅ **It came back rather than taking the easy read, because I had written "come back rather than default to the SKIP" into the authorisation** — the invitation earning its keep for the second time today. 🔑 **SAME STREAM, OPPOSITE ERRORS, WITHIN AN HOUR: its bug folds stderr INTO a compared value; mine in `daily_sweep.sh` threw stderr AWAY with `2>/dev/null`, so a failure could only report "unreadable".** Both are a control that cannot say what happened. 🔴 **AND THE FIND UNDER THE BUG IS BIGGER THAN THE BUG: the job whose purpose is checking for vulnerabilities fetches an UNPINNED package from the registry mid-run and executes it.** Authorised the pin and required it be ticketed as a supply-chain property, **not as "a dev tool was missing" — that framing is how it gets closed as housekeeping.** | w=1 (specifying a fix from an error message instead of the code) | Authorised the capture fix with BOTH arms required in the red-proof: stderr-present-and-NULL must pass, genuine-driver-failure must still fail. A one-armed proof would loosen the guard rather than fix it. |
| 2026-09-10 | 🔴 **A DEPLOY KAM AUTHORISED RAN CLEANLY, RETURNED FIVE GREEN SIGNALS, AND CHANGED NOTHING — and the ONLY reason we know is Kam's own outcome criterion.** KS-1041 Step 1 on the demo host: uploaded sha matched local, `nginx -t` **exit 0 before** the reload, `nginx -s reload` **exit 0**, container `Up 2 days (healthy)` (a reload, not a restart), rollback taken and byte-identical. **Every one of those is true and none of them is a fact about what the service is serving.** Half 1 of Kam's pair — *"the credential-free GET must return 404"* — came back **401 FROM ORIGINATE**, proving the route was still proxied there. **The exposure was never closed.** 🔑 CAUSE, measured to the inode: **a single-FILE bind mount binds the INODE, not the path.** Host `ae4cc54c` inode 1311680 vs container `265b7a60` inode 1311687; `cp` wrote a new inode and unlinked the old, the mount still resolved to the old one, nginx faithfully re-read the PREVIOUS config. Proven with a control: `find -inum 1311687` → **0 paths**, `find -inum 1311680` → its path. **No host-side write can reach that container.** ⚠ **THE CHEAP-PATH CLAIM WAS THE BASIS OF KAM'S AUTHORISATION AND IT WAS WRONG.** The seat told me "bind-mounted, so rsync + reload"; I passed it to Kam as the cheap-and-reversible framing. **My provenance line said I had not read the demo host and was relying on the seat — correct, and not a defence.** ✅ **The seat STOPPED at the boundary rather than reading `verify-now` as covering a container recreate**, which his ruling explicitly excluded — that is the rule holding under pressure, in the one situation where stretching it would have been easy and would have worked. Filed as [[2026-09-10_a-single-file-bind-mount-binds-the-inode]]. | w=1 new mechanism; the "check aimed at the wrong property" family it joins is high | Lesson filed. RULE ADDED: a deploy is verified by the BEHAVIOUR of the running service, never by the success of the steps meant to change it — and a premise that turns out false does not silently widen the authority built on it. |

| 2026-09-10 | 🟢 **MY OWN RED-PROOF HARNESS CAUGHT A COMMIT-DESTROYING BUG IN MY OWN FIX, BEFORE IT SHIPPED — and reading the diff would never have found it.** Fixing `panel_sync.sh`'s recovery, I detected "the replayed commit is empty" by grepping `rebase --continue`'s output for `--skip`. **git's CONFLICT output contains `You can instead skip this commit: run "git rebase --skip"`** — so on a CONFLICT, the one state where skipping is catastrophic, the detector matched and ran `git rebase --skip`, **silently dropping a real-work commit.** Found within seconds of running the real script against an induced conflict in a two-seat harness (bare origin + two clones); **invisible in the code, because the bug lives in the conjunction of my file and git's output text, which is not in my file.** Filed as [[2026-09-10_a-detector-keyed-on-remedy-text-matches-the-hint]]: key a detector on the CONDITION (`diff --diff-filter=U` empty — a fact about the repo) not on the REMEDY'S NAME, and ORDER the checks so the dangerous branch is unreachable from the dangerous state. **A recovery path is exactly the code that never runs in normal operation, so it is exactly the code that ships untested unless failure is induced deliberately.** | w=1 new root cause; the parent family (a-check-that-cannot-fail) is w=high | Lesson filed; the harness itself is the mechanism and it ran BEFORE the ship, which is the part to keep. 9 assertions, plus the OLD code run against the identical scenario as a negative control. |

Tuesday told to pull AND restart, with the in-memory reason stated. |

| 2026-09-10 | 🟠 **WEDNESDAY CREATED A TWENTY-MINUTE GIT TANGLE OUT OF A ONE-CARD PUSH, AND THE TANGLE IS WHAT RE-BROKE KAM'S CHANNEL.** A rejected push became: a conflicted rebase left mid-flight, a `checkout -B main` while that rebase was pending, a detached HEAD, a stale ref lock (*"cannot lock ref 'refs/heads/main': is at X but expected Y"*), and two `checkout -f` resets — one of which reverted the chat-stream repair. **Cost: ~20 minutes, one silently-undone fix on the principal's channel, and a decisions.json that had to be merged by hand under conservation assertions.** 🔑 **THE DIAGNOSIS IS NOT "know more git": every step was a reasonable response to the step before it, and the compounding came from acting on a REJECTED PUSH rather than stopping to read the topology.** The decisive facts — *ahead 5, behind 3*, *main is not a descendant of origin* — were one `status -sb` away throughout and were read only at the end. ✅ **What finally worked was the smallest possible move: save the ONE file that mattered to the scratchpad, hard-reset to origin, copy it back, commit, done.** ✅ **And what protected the data throughout was assertion, not care:** the union of both coordinators' cards was written only after asserting every id from both sides survived and no ruled card was downgraded. **RULE: on the SECOND consecutive push rejection, stop pushing and read the topology (`status -sb`, `merge-base --is-ancestor`). And when a repo is tangled, the recovery is to identify the smallest set of files that actually matter, preserve those OUT of the repo, and reset — not to keep negotiating with the history.** ⚠ **Standing: `panel_sync` pushes every 60s, so any hand push races it. Let the loop carry the commit unless it is urgent.** | correction | +1 (NEW: compounding git recovery from a rejected push, whose side effect silently reverted an unrelated repair) | [[2026-08-17_check-the-refusal-before-the-kill]] (**a refusal must be CONSUMED — I kept acting past rejections instead of reading them**) · [[2026-08-26_never-delete-cleanup-means-quarantine]] (the safety copies are why nothing was lost) · [[2026-09-06_other-projects-repos-are-read-only-git-verbs-that-write]] (the sibling: git verbs that write) · [[2026-08-14_i-read-representations-they-read-sources]] (a push's exit code is a representation of the topology) | **Nothing lost: decisions 217 with both coordinators' cards and all 211 rulings conserved and verified at origin; Kam's 122 messages intact; the lesson, ledger and daily note all confirmed on origin with a negative control.** |

You've got my approval."*** **THE TENSION IS REAL AND WAS NOT RESOLVED BY PICKING THE EASY SIDE: the 2026-08-03 read-only tracker grant says in terms *"no state changes... writes remain with each project's own agent under its own identity"*, and a PR base change is a state change.** Against that: he asked directly, pre-approved the answer, the action is reversible, and launching a whole agent session to change two dropdown values is disproportionate to the boundary it protects. **Done, and DISCLOSED to him as a scoped exception with the alternative offered back — *"if you would rather I had launched an agent to do it instead, say so and that is what I will do next time"* — rather than letting the precedent set itself quietly.** **Verified by RE-READING both from the API rather than trusting the PATCH response: #916's head `584b12ba1eed` matches exactly what s158 reported pushing, so the retarget moved no code.** Re-swept after: 1 off-develop remaining, the live stack. | grant/exception | 1 (NEW: a standing read-only boundary crossed on the principal's explicit direction — recorded as an exception, not folded into the rule) | [[2026-08-03_grant-readonly-tracker-access]] (**the rule crossed — its hard limit 1 is *READ-ONLY, ever***) · [[2026-07-31_manage-dont-do]] (writes stay with the project's agent) · [[2026-08-07_protocol-v1.3-signed-delegation]] (**a grant is narrowed or widened by HIM; this was him widening it for two objects, and it is recorded so the next seat does not read it as general**) · [[2026-08-16_classification-is-the-field-that-grants-authority]] (*reversible* was the scope word, and it was established by the fact the change is a dropdown that can be set back) | **THE SCOPE, written down so a successor cannot inherit it as licence: this covers TWO NAMED PRs on 2026-09-09 and nothing else. | 2026-09-09 | 🔴 **WEDNESDAY TOLD KAM A PR WOULD RETARGET ITSELF, IT DID NOT, AND HE MERGED IT INTO A BRANCH THAT HAD ALREADY BEEN MERGED AWAY FROM — the repo stayed blocked while every instrument said success.** Handing Kam the manual-merge steps, Wednesday wrote *"nine one four has to go first, and only then does nine one five retarget to develop."* **GitHub retargets a PR when its base branch is DELETED. What Wednesday MEANT is *do not perturb what the gate is measuring*. **A live reproduction of KS-1018 would have driven logins against `:6882` — no restart, no rebuild, no push, and it would have tripped the limiter and corrupted the gate's own matrix exactly as a restart would.** The seat deferred it, said so ON THE TICKET rather than leaving it silently absent, and asked: *"I read your hold as covering this even though it literally names only restart and rebuild; tell me if that is tighter than you intended."* **THE CLASS: a hold expressed as an enumeration of ACTIONS can only ever cover the actions its author happened to think of, and the reader is left to infer the property.** This reader inferred correctly. The next one carries the same risk in the direction that does not announce itself — a permitted-because-unlisted action against a live measurement surface. **It is the census/frame family pointed at an INSTRUCTION rather than at a measurement: the verb list is a frame, and the frame was narrower than the claim.** Cost: zero, and the disclosure is what made it visible. | correction | 1 (NEW: a hold enumerating verbs instead of naming the property it protects) | [[2026-09-07_a-census-complete-over-a-frame-that-is-not]] (the parent — an enumeration complete over a frame nobody stated) · [[2026-08-16_classification-is-the-field-that-grants-authority]] (a hold decides what is permitted, so its scope is the field that grants authority) · [[2026-09-07_name-the-field-that-says-whose-it-is]] (a rule handed to an agent commissions a whole class of choices; here it under-commissioned one) · [[2026-08-13_headline-must-match-the-operative-case]] | owned to the seat at the HEAD of the reply as Wednesday's error, not framed as its catch. **RULE, sent and binding from here: a hold names the PROPERTY it protects, then gives verbs as examples — "while a gate is driving a surface, do not generate traffic against that surface: no probes, no logins, no suite runs, no restarts, no rebuilds, no pushes to the gated branch; if you need the surface, ask and I will sequence it."** |
| 2026-09-09 | 🟡 **SELF-CAUGHT BY MY OWN NEGATIVE CONTROL: the push VERIFICATION returned eight false zeros, because `G="git -C …"` then `$G show` DOES NOT WORD-SPLIT IN ZSH — no command ran, and every check read 0.** The verification was the good discipline (`git show HEAD:<path>` on each artefact rather than trusting `HEAD == origin`, which is the 09-08 w=91 rule) and the INSTRUMENT was broken, so a clean push would have read as a completely failed one. **Caught in one step by the NEGATIVE CONTROL that was in the same block by habit: it returned 0 as required — and so did everything else, which is the all-fail tell.** Re-run with literal commands: 1/0/1/1/1/0/1/1 with a positive control proving the read works and the negative control still 0. **Same zsh trap as the 09-08 `for f in $IRREPLACEABLE_FILES` guard that could not detect a conflict marker — a variable holding a COMMAND is the new costume.** And it is the VOLUME condition named in last night’s retro (rules 19-21) firing on its first opportunity: eight cheap checks in one block, each justifying a hand-rolled instrument. **The direction was the harmless one this time — a false alarm about my own push rather than a false clean — but the same broken block would have reported a REAL failure as fine.** | correction | +1 (a-false-absence-is-usually-my-own-instrument; the ZSH-VARIABLE-AS-COMMAND costume, and rules 19-21’s volume condition on its first outing) | [[2026-09-08_a-false-absence-is-usually-my-own-instrument]] (rule 2: when every case fails identically, stop testing the subject and test the instrument — this is what caught it) · [[2026-08-26_zsh-has-no-pipestatus]] (the same shell, a new costume: word-splitting, not PIPESTATUS) · [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] | self-caught in one step; re-run literal, all eight verified with both controls. **RULE: never put a command in a shell variable in this tool — zsh will not split it. Write the command literally in every cell, however repetitive.** |

RULED BY KAM, NOT YET IN AN ARTEFACT` and was refused, and the card list was written as a run-on paragraph rather than the `- <card-id>: "<ruling>" -> <artefact>` shape the refusal text specifies. **THE DIAGNOSIS THE FOURTH INSTANCE OWES, and it is NOT "read the ledger harder" — this seat DID read it, whole, at boot:** the rule is about a LITERAL STRING and the failure is a FORMATTING REFLEX. PROVENANCE`, 2+3 = 09-08) | [[2026-08-09_an-enforcement-you-must-arm-is-not-one]] (in-path enforcement caught it a fourth time, free every time — which is exactly why it recurs) · [[2026-08-13_headline-must-match-the-operative-case]] (a rule retrievable at boot is not retrievable at the keyboard) · [[2026-08-03_workflow-systemisation-duty]] (the BRIEF TEMPLATE carrying the gate’s literal headings — named as a candidate on 09-07 and 09-08 and still unbuilt) | gate-caught pre-send, nothing launched at the refusal (`brief_and_launch.sh` sequencing holding); fixed and re-sent, verified at destination 20:10:15Z, launched `%5`. **BUILT AND RED-PROOFED THE SAME HOUR, not named and deferred a third time: `fleet/specs/BRIEF_SKELETON.md`.** ⚠ **AND THIS ROW AS FIRST WRITTEN OVERCLAIMED — it said "the four gate literals". `send_brief.sh` greps THREE (`^PROVENANCE:` :142 · `^SELF-CHECK: re-read end-to-end for contradictions \| <stamp>` :359 · `^RULED BY KAM, NOT YET IN AN ARTEFACT` :425, conditional on undelivered cards). `RULED BY WEDNESDAY FOR THIS PROJECT, STILL OPERATIVE` is a RULE whose enforcement is still only a candidate — corrected in place after READING the gate rather than composing from memory, which is the same defect this row is about, one layer up.** **THE SKELETON WAS REFUSED BY THE GATE ON ITS OWN FIRST RUN, and that is the best thing about it:** its explanatory comment SPELLED OUT the scope words, so the template tripped the guard it documents — a document about a hazard carrying the hazard, the NUL-byte-brief and leak-check-matching-its-own-comment class.
